:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: {.astro-f44q3k6v role="main" pagefind-body="" lang="en" dir="ltr"}
:::: {.content-panel .astro-2wc2n45z style="--borderTop: initial;--pageTitleDisplay: block;"}
::: {.sl-container .astro-2wc2n45z style="--borderTop: initial;--pageTitleDisplay: block;"}
# BigDecimal {#_top .astro-np5lzwrf}
:::
::::

::::::::: {.content-panel .astro-2wc2n45z style="--borderTop: initial;--pageTitleDisplay: block;"}
:::::::: {.sl-container .astro-2wc2n45z style="--borderTop: initial;--pageTitleDisplay: block;"}
::::::: sl-markdown-content
In JavaScript, numbers are typically stored as 64-bit floating-point
values. While floating-point numbers are fast and versatile, they can
introduce small rounding errors. These are often hard to notice in
everyday usage but can become problematic in areas like finance or
statistics, where small inaccuracies may lead to larger discrepancies
over time.

By using the BigDecimal module, you can avoid these issues and perform
calculations with a higher degree of precision.

The `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} data type can represent real numbers with a
large number of decimal places, preventing the common errors of
floating-point math (for example, 0.1 + 0.2 ≠ 0.3).

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h2 tabindex="-1"}
## How BigDecimal Works

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#how-bigdecimal-works){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="how-bigdecimal-works"}
:::

A `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} represents a number using two components:

1.  `value`{dir="auto"}: A `BigInt`{dir="auto"} that stores the digits
    of the number.
2.  `scale`{dir="auto"}: A 64-bit integer that determines the position
    of the decimal point.

The number represented by a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} is calculated as:
value x 10^-scale^.

- If `scale`{dir="auto"} is zero or positive, it specifies the number of
  digits to the right of the decimal point.
- If `scale`{dir="auto"} is negative, the `value`{dir="auto"} is
  multiplied by 10 raised to the power of the negated scale.

For example:

- A `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} with `value = 12345n`{dir="auto"} and
  `scale = 2`{dir="auto"} represents `123.45`{dir="auto"}.
- A `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} with `value = 12345n`{dir="auto"} and
  `scale = -2`{dir="auto"} represents `1234500`{dir="auto"}.

The maximum precision is large but not infinite, limited to 2^63^
decimal places.

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h2 tabindex="-1"}
## Creating a BigDecimal

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#creating-a-bigdecimal){.anchor-link

:::

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h3 tabindex="-1"}
### make

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#make){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="make"}
:::

The `make`{dir="auto"} function creates a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} by
specifying a `BigInt`{dir="auto"} value and a scale. The
`scale`{dir="auto"} determines the number of digits to the right of the
decimal point.

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::
::::::::

[console]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[.]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[[]{.twoslash-hover}]{.twoslash
style="--0:#6F42C1;--1:#B392F0"}

::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@since]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[v0.1.100]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs

::::::::::::::::::::::: code
[[]{.twoslash-hover}]{.twoslash style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}

:::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::
::::::::

[console]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[.]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[[]{.twoslash-hover}]{.twoslash
style="--0:#6F42C1;--1:#B392F0"}

::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

[v0.1.100]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: twoslash-popup-docs
Formats a given `BigDecimal` as a `string`.

If the scale of the `BigDecimal` is greater than or equal to 16, the
`BigDecimal` will be formatted in scientific notation.
:::

<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>import * as assert from &quot;node:assert&quot;import { format, unsafeFromString } from &quot;effect/BigDecimal&quot;
assert.deepStrictEqual(format(unsafeFromString(&quot;-5&quot;)), &quot;-5&quot;)assert.deepStrictEqual(format(unsafeFromString(&quot;123.456&quot;)), &quot;123.456&quot;)assert.deepStrictEqual(format(unsafeFromString(&quot;-0.00000123&quot;)), &quot;-0.00000123&quot;)</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

The `fromBigInt`{dir="auto"} function creates a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"}
from a `bigint`{dir="auto"}. The `scale`{dir="auto"} defaults to
`0`{dir="auto"}, meaning the number has no fractional part.

**Example** (Creating a BigDecimal from a BigInt)

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame not-content">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>1import { import BigDecimal</code></pre>
</figure>

[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.

### fromString

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#fromstring){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="fromstring"}

Parses a numerical string into a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"}. Returns an
`Option<BigDecimal>`{dir="auto"}:

- `Some(BigDecimal)`{dir="auto"} if the string is valid.
- `None`{dir="auto"} if the string is invalid.

**Example** (Parsing a String into a BigDecimal)

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

### unsafeFromString

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#unsafefromstring){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="unsafefromstring"}

The `unsafeFromString`{dir="auto"} function is a variant of
`fromString`{dir="auto"} that throws an error if the input string is
invalid. Use this only when you are confident that the input will always
be valid.

**Example** (Unsafe Parsing of a String)

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame not-content">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>1import { import BigDecimal</code></pre>

Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

### unsafeFromNumber

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon

numbers (`NaN`{dir="auto"}, `+Infinity`{dir="auto"}, or
`-Infinity`{dir="auto"}).

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

arguments are all passed to

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>

::: twoslash-popup-docs
Creates a `BigDecimal` from a `number` value.

It is not recommended to convert a floating point number to a decimal
directly, as the floating point representation may be unexpected.

Throws a `RangeError` if the number is not finite (`NaN`, `+Infinity` or
`-Infinity`).
:::

:::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@example]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name}
[]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}

<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>import * as assert from &quot;node:assert&quot;import { unsafeFromNumber, make } from &quot;effect/BigDecimal&quot;
assert.deepStrictEqual(unsafeFromNumber(123), make(123n, 0))assert.deepStrictEqual(unsafeFromNumber(123.456), make(123456n, 3))</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

[\@since]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[3.11.0]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h2 tabindex="-1"}
## Basic Arithmetic Operations

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#basic-arithmetic-operations){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="basic-arithmetic-operations"}
:::

The BigDecimal module supports a variety of arithmetic operations that
provide precision and avoid the rounding errors common in standard
JavaScript arithmetic. Below is a list of supported operations:

  Function                        Description
  ------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  `sum`{dir="auto"}               Adds two `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} values.
  `subtract`{dir="auto"}          Subtracts one `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value from another.
  `multiply`{dir="auto"}          Multiplies two `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} values.
  `divide`{dir="auto"}            Divides one `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value by another, returning an `Option<BigDecimal>`{dir="auto"}.
  `unsafeDivide`{dir="auto"}      Divides one `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value by another, throwing an error if the divisor is zero.
  `negate`{dir="auto"}            Negates a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value (i.e., changes its sign).
  `remainder`{dir="auto"}         Returns the remainder of dividing one `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value by another, returning an `Option<BigDecimal>`{dir="auto"}.
  `unsafeRemainder`{dir="auto"}   Returns the remainder of dividing one `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value by another, throwing an error if the divisor is zero.
  `sign`{dir="auto"}              Returns the sign of a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} value (`-1`{dir="auto"}, `0`{dir="auto"}, or `1`{dir="auto"}).
  `abs`{dir="auto"}               Returns the absolute value of a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"}.

**Example** (Performing Basic Arithmetic with BigDecimal)

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame not-content">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>1import { import BigDecimal</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither

they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::
::::::::

[console]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[.]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[[]{.twoslash-hover}]{.twoslash
style="--0:#6F42C1;--1:#B392F0"}

::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@since]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[v0.1.100]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>const divide: (self: BigDecimal.BigDecimal, that: BigDecimal.BigDecimal) =&gt; Option&lt;BigDecimal.BigDecimal&gt; (+1 overload)</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::: twoslash-popup-docs
Provides a division operation on `BigDecimal`s.

If the dividend is not a multiple of the divisor the result will be a
`BigDecimal` value which represents the integer division rounded down to
the nearest integer.

If the divisor is `0`, the result will be `None`.
:::

:::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@example]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name}
[]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>import * as assert from &quot;node:assert&quot;import { BigDecimal, Option } from &quot;effect&quot;
assert.deepStrictEqual(BigDecimal.divide(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;6&quot;), BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;)), Option.some(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;2&quot;)))assert.deepStrictEqual(BigDecimal.divide(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;6&quot;), BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;4&quot;)), Option.some(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;1.5&quot;)))assert.deepStrictEqual(BigDecimal.divide(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;6&quot;), BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;0&quot;)), Option.none())</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

[\@since]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[2.0.0]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
::::
:::::::

for more information.

::: expressive-code

::: twoslash-popup-docs
Provides an unsafe division operation on `BigDecimal`s.

If the dividend is not a multiple of the divisor the result will be a
`BigDecimal` value which represents the integer division rounded down to
the nearest integer.

Throws a `RangeError` if the divisor is `0`.

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.

:::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: twoslash-popup-docs
Returns the remainder left over when one operand is divided by a second
operand.

Throws a `RangeError` if the divisor is `0`.

assert.deepStrictEqual(unsafeRemainder(unsafeFromString(&quot;2&quot;), unsafeFromString(&quot;2&quot;)), unsafeFromString(&quot;0&quot;))assert.deepStrictEqual(unsafeRemainder(unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;), unsafeFromString(&quot;2&quot;)), unsafeFromString(&quot;1&quot;))assert.deepStrictEqual(unsafeRemainder(unsafeFromString(&quot;-4&quot;), unsafeFromString(&quot;2&quot;)), unsafeFromString(&quot;0&quot;))</code></pre>

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h2 tabindex="-1"}
## Comparison Operations

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#comparison-operations){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="comparison-operations"}
:::

The `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} module provides several functions for
comparing decimal values. These allow you to determine the relative
order of two values, find the minimum or maximum, and check specific
properties like positivity or integer status.

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h3 tabindex="-1"}
### Comparison Functions

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#comparison-functions){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="comparison-functions"}
:::

  Function                             Description
  ------------------------------------ --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  `lessThan`{dir="auto"}               Checks if the first `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} is smaller than the second.
  `lessThanOrEqualTo`{dir="auto"}      Checks if the first `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} is smaller than or equal to the second.
  `greaterThan`{dir="auto"}            Checks if the first `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} is larger than the second.
  `greaterThanOrEqualTo`{dir="auto"}   Checks if the first `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} is larger than or equal to the second.
  `min`{dir="auto"}                    Returns the smaller of two `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} values.
  `max`{dir="auto"}                    Returns the larger of two `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} values.

**Example** (Comparing Two BigDecimal Values)

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::
::::::::

[console]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[.]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[[]{.twoslash-hover}]{.twoslash
style="--0:#6F42C1;--1:#B392F0"}

::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@since]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[v0.1.100]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

assert.deepStrictEqual(lessThan(unsafeFromString(&quot;2&quot;), unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;)), true)assert.deepStrictEqual(lessThan(unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;), unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;)), false)assert.deepStrictEqual(lessThan(unsafeFromString(&quot;4&quot;), unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;)), false)</code></pre>

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to

- A global `console` instance configured to write to

  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the

for more information.

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3

:::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::
::::::::

[console]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[.]{style="--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8"}[[]{.twoslash-hover}]{.twoslash
style="--0:#6F42C1;--1:#B392F0"}

::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@since]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[v0.1.100]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

### Predicates for Comparison

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#predicates-for-comparison){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="predicates-for-comparison"}
:::

The module also includes predicates to check specific properties of a
`BigDecimal`{dir="auto"}:

  Predicate                  Description
  -------------------------- ----------------------------------------------------------------
  `isZero`{dir="auto"}       Checks if the value is exactly zero.
  `isPositive`{dir="auto"}   Checks if the value is positive.
  `isNegative`{dir="auto"}   Checks if the value is negative.
  `between`{dir="auto"}      Checks if the value lies within a specified range (inclusive).
  `isInteger`{dir="auto"}    Checks if the value is an integer (i.e., no fractional part).

**Example** (Checking the Sign and Properties of BigDecimal Values)

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)

[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.

assert.deepStrictEqual(between(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;3&quot;)), true)assert.deepStrictEqual(between(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;0&quot;)), false)assert.deepStrictEqual(between(BigDecimal.unsafeFromString(&quot;6&quot;)), false)</code></pre>

:::::::::::::: code

:::::::: {.twoslash-popup-container .not-content}
[]{.twoslash-popup-code-type}

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

::::: twoslash-popup-docs
The `console` module provides a simple debugging console that is similar
to the JavaScript console mechanism provided by web browsers.

The module exports two specific components:

- A `Console` class with methods such as `console.log()`,
  `console.error()` and `console.warn()` that can be used to write to
  any Node.js stream.
- A global `console` instance configured to write to
  [`process.stdout`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstdout)
  and
  [`process.stderr`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#processstderr).
  The global `console` can be used without importing the `node:console`
  module.

***Warning***: The global console object\'s methods are neither
consistently synchronous like the browser APIs they resemble, nor are
they consistently asynchronous like all other Node.js streams. See the
[`note on process I/O`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/process.html#a-note-on-process-io)
for more information.

Example using the global `console`:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>console.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to stdoutconsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints error message and stack trace to stderr://   Error: Whoops, something bad happened//     at [eval]:5:15//     at Script.runInThisContext (node:vm:132:18)//     at Object.runInThisContext (node:vm:309:38)//     at node:internal/process/execution:77:19//     at [eval]-wrapper:6:22//     at evalScript (node:internal/process/execution:76:60)//     at node:internal/main/eval_string:23:3
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;console.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to stderr</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

Example using the `Console` class:

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const out = getStreamSomehow();const err = getStreamSomehow();const myConsole = new console.Console(out, err);
myConsole.log(&#39;hello world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.log(&#39;hello %s&#39;, &#39;world&#39;);// Prints: hello world, to outmyConsole.error(new Error(&#39;Whoops, something bad happened&#39;));// Prints: [Error: Whoops, something bad happened], to err
const name = &#39;Will Robinson&#39;;myConsole.warn(`Danger ${name}! Danger!`);// Prints: Danger Will Robinson! Danger!, to err</code></pre>
</figure>
:::
:::::

::: {.twoslash-popup-docs .twoslash-popup-docs-tags}
[\@see]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-name} ―
[[source](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v22.x/lib/console.js)]{.twoslash-popup-docs-tag-value}
:::

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>Console.log(message?: any, ...optionalParams: any[]): void</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

:::: twoslash-popup-docs
Prints to `stdout` with newline. Multiple arguments can be passed, with
the first used as the primary message and all additional used as
substitution values similar to
[`printf(3)`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/printf.3.html) (the
arguments are all passed to
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)).

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="js"><code>const count = 5;console.log(&#39;count: %d&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdoutconsole.log(&#39;count:&#39;, count);// Prints: count: 5, to stdout</code></pre>
</figure>
:::

See
[`util.format()`](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/util.html#utilformatformat-args)
for more information.
::::

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h2 tabindex="-1"}
## Normalization and Equality

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon
aria-hidden="true"}](index.html#normalization-and-equality){.anchor-link
aria-labelledby="normalization-and-equality"}
:::

In some cases, two `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} values can have different
internal representations but still represent the same number.

For example, `1.05`{dir="auto"} could be internally represented with
different scales, such as:

- `105n`{dir="auto"} with a scale of `2`{dir="auto"}
- `1050n`{dir="auto"} with a scale of `3`{dir="auto"}

To ensure consistency, you can normalize a `BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} to
adjust the scale and remove trailing zeros.

::: {.autolink-heading-container .level-h3 tabindex="-1"}
### Normalization

[[![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyB3aWR0aD0iMTYiIGhlaWdodD0iMTYiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAyNCAyNCI+PHBhdGggZmlsbD0iY3VycmVudGNvbG9yIiBkPSJtMTIuMTEgMTUuMzktMy44OCAzLjg4YTIuNTIgMi41MiAwIDAgMS0zLjUgMCAyLjQ3IDIuNDcgMCAwIDEgMC0zLjVsMy44OC0zLjg4YTEgMSAwIDAgMC0xLjQyLTEuNDJsLTMuODggMy44OWE0LjQ4IDQuNDggMCAwIDAgNi4zMyA2LjMzbDMuODktMy44OGExIDEgMCAxIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQyWm04LjU4LTEyLjA4YTQuNDkgNC40OSAwIDAgMC02LjMzIDBsLTMuODkgMy44OGExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS40MiAxLjQybDMuODgtMy44OGEyLjUyIDIuNTIgMCAwIDEgMy41IDAgMi40NyAyLjQ3IDAgMCAxIDAgMy41bC0zLjg4IDMuODhhMSAxIDAgMSAwIDEuNDIgMS40MmwzLjg4LTMuODlhNC40OSA0LjQ5IDAgMCAwIDAtNi4zM1pNOC44MyAxNS4xN2ExIDEgMCAwIDAgMS4xLjIyIDEgMSAwIDAgMCAuMzItLjIybDQuOTItNC45MmExIDEgMCAwIDAtMS40Mi0xLjQybC00LjkyIDQuOTJhMSAxIDAgMCAwIDAgMS40MloiIC8+PC9zdmc+)]{.anchor-icon

aria-labelledby="normalization"}

The `BigDecimal.normalize`{dir="auto"} function adjusts the scale of a
`BigDecimal`{dir="auto"} and eliminates any unnecessary trailing zeros
in its internal representation.

::: expressive-code
<figure class="frame">
<pre data-language="ts"><code>var console: Console</code></pre>
</figure>
